ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e5701	The fact that the financial sector itself must contribute to the stability of the financial market is undisputed. There's consensus about that. We then decided to grant this bank levy, which will contribute €120 million to a dedicated fund to stabilise the financial market, and this fund will be paid by 2017. 14 days ago, however, the ECOFIN Ministers in Europe also prepared a Banking Resolution Directive, which requires the banking sector itself to set up a fund of several billions in order to achieve these settlements. So if this directive is transposed, there will be an unlimited bank levy in this respect.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e11554	Madam President. Minister! Mr. State Secretary! Dear colleagues in plenary! Ladies and gentlemen in front of the TV! The purpose of this draft law is to better regulate and better manage the handling of banks in difficulty. The aim of the law is that no public funds are used to stabilise banks. It is also intended to allow banking supervision to intervene earlier if a bank has problems. This will be achieved by making preventive crisis planning by the banks and by bank supervision. Rehabilitation plans and resolution plans are to be drawn up and early intervention measures can be taken. A plan is to be drawn up on how to deal with crisis scenarios when a bank has problems. Solutions are to be drawn up. These plans and concepts must be transmitted annually to financial market supervision. The Austrian National Bank is to be involved in the examination of these plans as an expert. We liberals fear that this bill is a purely theoretical one that the law cannot be implemented in practice. [[Abg. May. Ikrath: Why are you afraid of that? I don't understand now!]] No sanctions are provided if the plans are not realistically drawn up if the plans do not work in practice. There is no way to account for the banks, how to account for the banks. [[New call for tender of the Abg. May. Ikrath.]] Financial market supervision and Oesterreichische Nationalbank as an expert: In the past, it has shown how helpless or ignorant they are. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] [[Abg. May. Ikrath: That's why you make the law, Mr. Colleague!]] Let's go back to Hypo Alpe-Adria. The Oesterreichische National Bank has still issued a best witness in autumn 2008 of the hypo, has put the outlook for 2009 on profit expectations and said: The bank is well placed and it works! [[Abg. May. Ikrath: Mr President, this is a case-by-case mistake!]] [[Abg. If you are unable to recognize in autumn 2008 that the bank is on the wrong track, then, I believe, the OeNB has no right, just like the supervision of the financial market, to examine these concepts! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We are convinced by the liberals that this law can do nothing except bureaucracy, except administrative expenditure. It is reasonable that there is a supervisory system in the European Union, a regulation for all banks, a control for banks, because there is also a market, and that one of course does not have 27 different rules, not 27 different supervisors who always see only a small part of the bank. We need a European solution here, and it is true that sometimes this does not go as fast as we all want at European level. However, it is right to create uniform European rules, especially as regards banks, because they are not now active in a country, but sometimes in ten different countries – and ten different bankruptcy rules for one and the same bank would really be absurd. It would also be better if we could decide on the complete package today, but it is nevertheless the right way to seek a European solution. What's going on today? – The fact that, as has already been said, banks have to prepare for what happens if they are economically ill: Which parts are “save” – under quotation marks – because there are deposits inside, what are the healthy parts and where are the risky parts? The second step, which is missing, is that in the end, not the State pays, but those who have borrowed money from the bank, have drawn the bonds by converting these bonds into equity. [[Abg. May. Kogler: But this is not regulated in the law!]] – But the preparations for this are in this law! I expressly say: The second step is what is still missing: that the debt of a bank’s deposit of money is virtually converted into voting capital, into bank’s capital, and thus the bank is sufficiently capitalised. Then we wouldn't have the problems we had at the hypo. For the hypo, it would have been wonderful if in the bonds it would not only have been said that the country of Carinthia is liable, which has led to an emergency state but if it were the same: In the case of bankruptcy, it doesn't pay you Carinthia, but then you hold your equity to the bank. – Then we would not have these problems today, and then we would not have needed the necessary nationalization. In this respect, this is another step – one of many, many steps – to draw the right lessons from the crisis. In any case, this draft law has the approval of Social Democracy. Thank you.

We have been doing well for many years with this separation banking system, in which we simply peel the playing banks out of the large corporations and separate them from the commercial banks. The commercial banks basically have a healthy structure, which are usually those banks we have to deal with in Austria, of which we still receive loans. These are the good banks, and we stand with them too, Mr Ikrath! But what we don't want to do for, are the playing banks, those banks that go to the casinos and are liable for the taxpayers. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] There must be no place for such banking models in our banking and financial system, and this must finally be understood. Of course, the major Group heads are opposed to this separation. I also admit that it is difficult to carry out this separation, but if we do not do it and do not finally come to this separation banking system, then the financial market will not work in the future either in Austria or at European level.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e12122	Now comes Mr Mag. State Secretary. Arbitrators to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e12143	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! What is now on the agenda, and the next items on the agenda, in total, represent a very huge regulatory package, a wealth of various detailed measures that seek to restore reasonable rules to the unbundled financial markets. These include the regulation of hedge fund managers, which will later be discussed and decided to increase equity for Austrian banks in this case – but over Basel III, this is so defined worldwide – as well as new corporate governance rules for banks and the bank directors and also a new framework for macro-ruddenial supervision. The central point of this entire package is the Banking Intervention and Restructuring Act or vulgo, as it is referred to in the Volksmund, banking insolvency law, what we are discussing here now. It is a fundamental step in Austrian legal and financial history, because for the first time, a legal framework is laid down to ensure that in the future, what we have felt so painful in the crisis, namely that banks are too big to fail, that we do not know how to deal with it, with the risks, systemic risks, the impact on the financial market, on the other institutions, on the people who have credit there, the companies, and all these questions, that it is not always the taxpayer who will have to jump in automatically in the future. It is about making a cut that a shaky position in a banking institution does not automatically result in the state, if you want to, standing with the back to the wall and saying, out of considerations of economic stability and others I can do nothing else. That is why we have been looking for a legal instrument to deal with it. The banking system is one that has been discussed. But you have to look at Austrian banking reality, Austrian banks are not investment banks. [[Abg. Bucher: Some of it!]] And as a counter-argument, one could even say: Lehman was a purely investment bank, quite classically organized by the banking system, and has argued. A global financial crisis has arisen. You have to see that too. Therefore, one must look for answers that are not so trivially simply oriented to a keyword, but that give us solutions: What do we do when – and there are two questions – first of all an institute gets in a state of disrepute? Therefore restructuring plans. This means that banks have to predetermine what in individual pre-defined cases – less equity, business areas break away, and, and, can be done or done, so that, if the case occurs, supervision can adopt and say a previously approved plan: Please convert points A, B, C from this plan! And the second question is to create a “testament”, to define what happens to an institution when it is pleading if it threatens to argue so that the supervision, the Ministry of Finance, the public and the bank themselves know: [[Abg. Bucher: The main inheritance is the taxpayer!]] What's the risk in there? What do we have to do now, so that society does not have to pay again, but the bank itself? These plans, which must be drawn up in advance of this “testament”, which banks have to prepare, must be updated annually, must also be approved by the Financial Market Supervisory Board – every year new. Financial market supervision can also, if it is dissatisfied with the plan and it has gaps, give improvement orders so that there is a reasonable system here. And it is also – and I think this is very, very important and necessary – to create group plans, so that small financial institutions, which are part of a large group, experience these solutions there too. What is even better, because then the group takes care of solving the problem and not the taxpayer or supervision. That's why it's about and it's about in the future that the taxpayer doesn't have to stand straight again and again when something happens. Now you could ask yourself the question: What would that have changed if we had already had it at the beginning of the crisis? And that's where I have to say: Strategically, we could have answered the case of Hypo differently, with the exception of the question of liability. Secondly, we could have answered the question of Volksbanken and the question of local credit differently, namely by early intervention of financial market supervision, which would not have triggered a bank run. It could most likely have intervened earlier. But I also admit that it is not the entire history of banking union. There is also the European part of the Banking Union. And this is about defining Bail-in-Instruments and also eating a resolution fund and finally feeding a deposit guarantee fund across Europe in a second step. And here we have deliberately decided not to make an Austrian single entry, as with the “testaments” and the insolvency law, but to wait for European agreement. For two reasons: first because it has already arrived. As you know, the ECOFIN has taken the decisions here in recent weeks. Now we are still seeking agreement with the European Parliament, and I expect that this year's decision will be taken on this issue. Secondly, because it is a question of finding Europe-wide market-based solutions. It makes no sense if Austria has a different regime than Germany and the Czech Republic and we have banks that are active in all these markets, because of course this is also capital costs. And here, Europe will bring exactly these proposals in the future. Austria has come on the very strict side, together with Germany and other countries, which provide for very strict and clear, comprehensible arrangements for the banking union. To sum up, therefore: I am very glad that it has succeeded in deciding on the normal session activity of this important instrument on the last day. We have been discussing this for a very long time, we have also been considering it for a very long time, because it was also a new country that had to be entered. But it is an important instrument that also shows – and that is, I believe, what you have to stress – that we have also removed the lessons from the crisis by improving the laws where it is necessary, or by creating new laws where we need them, so that in the future, when something goes wrong, we can no longer stand with the back to the wall, but act in good time. In this sense, I think this is a very, very good law, which also creates more independence from politics and government from the mistakes in the financial markets.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e13650	Mr President. Mrs Finanzminister! Ladies and gentlemen! We decide today a very comprehensive – State Secretary has said it – banking regulation package, which is on the table. This is a matter that has been discussed intensively over the last two or three years, with many negotiations, a lot of brain narrowness created, according to best knowledge and conscience, at various levels: at European level, in the trialogue between European Parliament, European Commission and European Council. In Austria there were a lot of discussions, many negotiations. Ladies and gentlemen, the central point is simply a clearer and clearer system of capital and liquidity for banks. With what objective? – With the aim of increasing financial stability, increasing resistance to crises and minimising the risk to taxpayers as much as possible. The problem, however, was – and it must be said quite openly – that the basic concept was actually placed on large international banks, which were primarily active in investment banking. That is to say, it was the great task of changing the very right idea so that it fits into the structure of our banking landscape and into our business models, ladies and gentlemen. We've heard over and over again, we don't have the big investment banks. We have banks that finance the real economy, ladies and gentlemen, banks that are the blood circulation of our economy without which we could not have a thriving economy. And I would also like to say one thing, since the banks have recently been set up as an enemy by certain political groups, ladies and gentlemen: We have over 800 banks with 75 000 employees in Austria. Three of these 800 banks have demanded the ownership of the state – three out of 800! And these 75 000 employees in the banks work daily according to best knowledge and conscience. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Krainer:Top 10]] And we do not allow a banking bashing to be carried out here in general and the banks are being deflated here. Jakob Auer: That's it!]] And I would like to admit that we also need additional forms of funding that are increasingly emerging. But that will undoubtedly be a topic. The whole issue of medium-sized finance is not only a challenge for banks themselves, for credit institutions, but also undoubtedly a challenge for the next Federal Government, because we have a medium-sized country that is increasingly complaining: Please, we're having a hard time with the loan money! Therefore, on the one hand, this complementary instrument, but on the other hand the challenge to banks. There were interviews yesterday with the bosses of the three largest banks, namely Raiffeisen, Erste and Bank Austria, where all three said: We are aware of the challenge, we must make our contribution to the financing of the economy! Ladies and gentlemen, I have already said this: All of Europe is lagging behind economic growth. We have growth potential in Austria, but we have two major growth brakes. The one growth brake is access, especially SMEs, to finance – therefore, please also the challenge for banks and the crowdfunding instrument. The second major growth brake is the shortage of skilled workers, ladies and gentlemen. That means: We have growth potential that we could use if we remove these brakes, the brakes on financing and also the brakes on the shortage of skilled workers. In my view, this is undoubtedly one of the very big challenges for the next legislative period. This also applies to the level of government, but also to Parliament. I thank my colleagues in the Financial Committee. We have, I believe, implemented a number of amendments which make this Basel III instrument compatible with our banking landscape, the structure of our credit institutions and ultimately our business models in Austria. I believe that it will be possible to live with it – undoubtedly a major challenge for banks, but in the interests of financial stability and in the interest of the possible protection of taxpayers in the event of crises.

The fact that the economic benefits are very high is quite obvious. Financing will secure businesses and create jobs. ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e16965	Mr President. High house! Because of the 'Twopack', the government debt committee is to be converted into the Fiscal Council. I've known this committee for 30 years. Until 2006, I was sitting in the committee itself, since then I have been an expert in the working committee. I can very much welcome this decision and welcome it in principle, also on the basis of my experience, that those members who will now be members of the Committee will be free of discretion. This is a very important point, but it must be lived. In this sense it is also to be welcomed that the period is extended from four to six years. The new Fiscal Council, which is to be set up, receives new tasks, fundamentally new tasks, namely the delivery of recommendations on medium-term budgetary targets, the delivery of recommendations on the budgetary adjustment path and the monitoring of compliance with the numerous fiscal rules. I don't know. Are they nine fiscal rules or something? – Insurmountable, very complex. This government template is basically in order, but it is too much for me. I therefore propose an amendment tabled by Mr Kogler, Mr Rossmann, and others, which I will explain in the broad outlines below. First of all, we want - we discussed this in the Committee - to include the House's budget service in this committee with an advisory vote, both in the Committee itself and in its potential work committees. That is contained in this amendment. In addition, I also believe that experts from those economic research institutes should be represented with an advisory voice to produce economic forecasts. Why is that so important? This is important, therefore, because the new tasks of this Fiscal Council now need sufficient information, even in terms of estimates of budget forecasts, structural deficits and the like, which are actually being done by the economic research institutes, which also produce forecasts. That is why, I believe, it makes sense for those research institutes to be represented by an advisory vote both in the Committee itself and in its work committees. A second important point, and we have also discussed it in the Committee, is the question of information to be sent to the Committee. It is necessary to ensure that the Fiscal Council is given adequate access to the necessary information to the spectrum of tasks it now has. And I am speaking from experience here because I know how difficult it is for the Committee secretariat to get the necessary information from the relevant bodies. These are in particular the Federal Government, countries, municipalities, but also Statistics Austria. That is why I have also included in this amendment a passage which not only calls for adequate access to information, but also explains in what areas of information flow need to be strengthened and who are the relevant contact persons to be involved in this task so that this new committee, which is to be set up, can fulfil its tasks in any way. This is about the fact that, without detailed information, a serious assessment of these many fiscal rules is not at all possible. This applies once to the quality of the data provided, but this also applies to the forecasts, to the economic forecasts, and this also applies with regard to the Estimate methods, particularly with regard to the methods of estimate of structural budget deficits, which are, in some way, put in the focus of these budget rules. Who knows that the forecasts on structural deficits can lead to very different results for the same time, at different times, and knows how important it is for this Committee to be provided with this information. And it is also important that the two research institutes, the economic forecasts and the forecasts on structural deficits are included in this Committee. The same applies, however, to provisional budget data. It must be borne in mind that, for example, I would just like to give you an example, the final data for the communities for 2012 only with the budgetary notification in March 2014. Therefore, we need provisional budgetary data from the relevant bodies – and this is Statistics Austria. And from my experience I know that not only Statistics Austria, but also the Federation, but also the countries and communities with the provision of information – how should I formulate it now? – to be restrained. It's still noblely formulated. If you want to, you can also say: Well, what are you doing? But then I come back to those principles that are enshrined in the Constitution – Article 51 para. 8 has already been mentioned – including the principle of transparency. And if all of this is not fulfilled, then this independent Fiscal Council will not be able to fulfil its new range of tasks, which it has been given along the way. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e18715	Madam President. Members of the Federal Government! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! On Question No 1, which addresses in particular the measures at European level within the framework of the European Union, it is to be said that I and the entire Federal Government will, of course, work towards comprehensive information. We also assume that these topics will be discussed and discussed in detail in the relevant specialist councils. The need for a common political and European approach is highlighted in the discussion on the one hand, but also the necessary agreement on the other. I am convinced that the position of those responsible in the countries of the European Union is identical and therefore a common determined procedure is necessary. Questions 2 and 3 relating to the assessment of the situation: I expect, as well as you, honourable Members, complete and rapid information from the U.S. authorities. We need and expect transparency from the US to restore lost confidence. In the interests of the United States, it is also to answer the questions we have asked. If the allegations are confirmed, these transactions should be considered totally unacceptable and should therefore be stopped immediately. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP and FPÖ.]] On questions 4, 9 and 11, concerning the termination of data exchange agreements: The purpose of the corresponding agreements is to place the data exchange on a legal basis and to ensure the rights of persons concerned. In our view, therefore, a notice of these agreements would not be appropriate. The last Council on Justice and Home Affairs agreed to set up an EU and US expert group to clarify, in particular, how the data of EU citizens concerned has been handled. A meeting, attended by representatives of the EU and the Member States, is scheduled to take place on 8 May. July. We are examining the extent to which Austrian citizens as well as companies were affected or may still be affected by these activities. On the basis of the results, appropriate measures will have to be discussed immediately in the Federal Government and, as mentioned above, to be adopted in close coordination with our partners in the EU in order to ensure a Community procedure. On question 5 concerning the Free Trade Agreement: In my view, such an agreement should be reviewed and assessed as to whether it actually creates and secures jobs in Austria and Europe and what the benefits for consumers are. I see quite independently of these incidents some open questions about the Free Trade Agreement, especially when it comes to the fact that we do not want to adopt European standards on food law. If, for example, we want to maintain the Austrian attitude towards genetic engineering products, which we do not want, into genetically modified foods and also to maintain the European standards, which are partly agreed in part, we must not, of course, be threatened by a free trade agreement. A number of environmental and social issues related to this Free Trade Agreement, as well as its advantages and disadvantages, must be examined. On questions 6 to 8, 10, 12 and 13 which concern the institutions, the damage and the information I have already mentioned several times: The Ministry of Foreign Affairs contacted the U.S. Embassy in Vienna immediately after the so-called PRISM programme became known and requested information on this programme and possible consultation measures in Austria. As more and more elements of this programme have become known, particularly with regard to the interception of EU institutions and institutions in the EU Member States through the media, US ambassador William Eacho became president on 1 January. In July, he was invited to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, where he met for a 30-minute discussion with Mr. Vice-Chancellor. In this conversation, the Vice-Chancellor repeated the Austrian demands for rapid information and full of information by the United States. In this context, the Vice-Chancellor also referred to the catalogue of questions on the NSA monitoring programme drawn up by the Ministry of Interior and recalled that the US replies were still pending, whether the personal data of Austrian citizens or companies were collected. Ambassador Eacho agreed to refer the Austrian concerns to the competent authorities in Washington again. Questions 14 and 15 concern legal action: The competent authorities and services will, of course, assist companies concerned in securing their systems and implementing security rules and will also provide them with support in the pursuit of legal claims in a consular manner. On the question 16: The Austrian asylum procedure is a state-of-the-art and, as you know, requires the competent authorities and courts to examine each case individually. The granting of asylum without prior examination of the case would therefore be a clear violation of existing Austrian law from our point of view. On the question 17, over-flight ban: The granting of overflight rights is in principle within the competence of the individual Member States and is therefore to be clarified bilaterally in a diplomatic way. However, I would like to stress that the competent authorities in Austria – that is to say, primarily the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, but also the Austro Control – responded quickly and professionally when the Bolivian President’s plane sought a land permit. On the question 18: I would just like to tell you that the Ministry of Defence has given me the information that there is no connection.

That is in no way to justify what has happened, namely that the US has been particularly involved in these fundamental and freedom rights in the most massive way. And that the proportionality between security issues, protection against terrorism and the privacy of people is in no way given, that is, as I believe, obvious. I think that when we look into history and into our closer neighborhood, we see what has happened there, we also know that only the knowledge of the people about the possibility of one being stifled, that one is monitored, that one is scanned, in the truest sense of the word, that only the knowledge of these possibilities can startle crazy fear and distrust and destroy an entire society. I refer to the GDR and to what happened there, where the neighbor was afraid of being a neighbor, where in some cases in the families the family members no longer trusted each other. That should have been enough for us to teach us that such things – with fundamental and freedom rights, with privacy, with the protection of personal life – have to be particularly sensitive and particularly careful, ladies and gentlemen. I miss this sensitivity very much in this discussion. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] I also believe that it is important to discuss, and I would like to introduce another aspect, namely that we also need to discuss more closely, for example, the question of net neutrality, where there is an urgent need for legislation. It is also necessary to discuss whether it would not be targeted, as the other states like China or Russia are already operating, to become independent not only in questions of energy or other things, but also in questions of the Internet and the World Wide Web. The fact is that the entire Internet, the entire network depends on services and input from the USA and that all central Internet companies are in American hands. The fact is that we know today that the big Internet companies like Google, like Facebook, like Apple, like Microsoft not only lied to the public, but also gave US security authorities extensive access to all data. It must be our interest, ladies and gentlemen, to become independent of these U.S. Internet providers and thus also to stop the means of monitoring. I also believe it is important that appropriate initiatives be taken at European level. This discussion is at all an occasion for me to say, in general, that the role of the USA as a whole and this constant replay of this country as the democratic world politician is quite nervous. The fact is that the US has proved not only with the current surveillance, but with a variety of actions they have committed, that they themselves have a massive need for action in the area of human rights and democracy. I refer to the case of Manning, ladies and gentlemen. We know today that he, who has also very, very much revealed, was taken the right to a rule of law correct procedure by the United States, that this person was tortured for months, that he was forbidden to contact his lawyer, that he was held in isolation, that he was mentally broken, that he had been subjected to body visits, and that, not least, on the basis of American law, he also threatened the death penalty, ladies and gentlemen. Only the fact that a country continues to apply the death penalty is enough that in my opinion such a country has done any right to demand democracy and correct conditions in the world. The death penalty is unacceptable, and it is therefore also important that measures be taken here so that Mr Snowden does not get the same fate as Mr Manning. It is therefore to be welcomed in principle and important for Austria to be given the opportunity of asylum, even though I must say that it is not so uncritical to see the role of Mr Snowden – I am in favour of asylum law, as I said – as it is. There a 30-year-old says he has planned to reveal everything from the beginning, then he gives up everything, including his family, and flees to China or Hong Kong, which belongs to China today. There he was certainly not only received with a bouquet of flowers, and that he was allowed to continue from there to Moscow, no one will believe that, because Mr. Snowden had five laptops in his luggage, with numerous data on it, and the Chinese will be very, very interested. The fact that he has provided them with comprehensive information is to be expected. That he will then be the second main competitor of the USA – let's put it that way! – that is, to continue to fly to Russia, which is also a question that needs to be discussed. So I am also in favour of illuminating Mr Snowden's role and not completely uncritically discussing it. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] However, this does not change the fact that we consider the possibility of asylum to be very, very important here in neutral Austria. This has been done in a rule of law In fact, procedures, as mentioned by Pilz, are to be carried out in order to clarify the role of Mr Snowden objectively and correctly.

Brosz.]] We have done two things with the reform of administrative jurisdiction or with the creation of administrative jurisdiction: on the one hand, a simplification, so instead of 120 special authorities, we have established nine national administrative courts and two administrative courts, with 1. ==References====External links== At the same time, it is probably the largest rule of law reform in this Second Republic. I've said it several times: This was referred to by many experts as a great throw, as a century reform and so on. We have made a reform in the field of foreign and asylum, where instead of 194, we have created an authority, namely the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum. We have negotiated a security reform, and we have also decided here where nine national police agencies have been set up instead of 31 authorities. We have set up an administrative reform in the field of care funds, where instead of 303 institutions responsible, five institutions have been decided. We have faster procedures now. This is praised by all sides. We have negotiated and decided on deregulation measures together with the federal states. We have adopted a Stability Pact with the countries. We have decided on a consolidation package, which we also spoke about yesterday, which is praised by the European Commission and by the OECD – the honourable Member has also mentioned the International Monetary Fund. We have a health reform with the countries, with social security companies negotiating with common objective control, with a view to ensuring that health care is more efficient, better coordinated and better functioning in the sense of patients. After years of discussion, we decided ELGA, the Electronic Health Act. We have decided on the limits of liability. We have decided to strengthen community cooperation across the national borders. The school administration reform has already been discussed. And we have decided on many more individual projects, which I do not list individually now, but which also implement much of what the Court of Auditors has listed in this brochure. I would also like to take this opportunity to thank all those involved in the decision-making process before the summer break, including all these projects, which have contributed to the realization of the democratic art of the feasible. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e23792	Mr Dipl.-Ing. Berlakovich. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e23813	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The Environmental Control Report is prepared every three years. This is the tenth environmental report and I thank the experts of the Federal Environment Agency for the detailed data collection, for the recommendations. This Tenth Environmental Control Report is a very good testimonie to the environment in Austria as a whole. Finally, this has been confirmed by two renowned American universities, which rank Austria as 7 out of more than 130 states, reaching environmental quality. In other words, we have made great progress in many areas. This was also noted positively in the Environmental Control Report. But of course we also need action. For example, water quality has developed positively. To provide people with sufficient drinking water, in sufficient quantity, in high quality. We have a very positive response to flood protection, including waste management, resource management and renewable energy, and Mr Hornek has just mentioned it. We need action in terms of climate protection and soil consumption. Without going too much into the detail now, only a few aspects: The water quality, drinking water quality and also bathing water quality in the rivers and lakes is excellent. That's also confirmed by all the investigations. Especially now when summer becomes one again, it is important for the people who want to use it. This was last confirmed by the European Environment Agency. There is a need for improvement in water ecology. Water ecology means that, for example, permeability is not guaranteed for fish, including because of hydroelectric power plants, but also flood protection plants. That is why we adopted the National Water Management Plan in 2009 together with the federal states, NGOs and the energy industry. Train around train we build fish ascent aids at large power plants, but also at flood protection plants. So far, we have implemented 110 projects. In the coming years, this needs to be consistently expanded to improve water ecology – that is, not water quality, which is high – in order to improve the continuity of the rivers. For the implementation of the Flood Framework Directives: We're also very good on the road. Especially the floodwaters of recent weeks have shown that we have a high degree of expansion, which leads to protection. We have been operating ecological flood protection for several years. That means we give the river flood room, retention room. In recent years there have been some 8,000 hectares at the Inn, at Lech, at Drau, at Mur, at the Danube, at Lafnitz, at Leitha, at various rivers. And we're going to expand this too. We have recently decided in the Federal Government on the topic of high-water safe Austria a plan where, among other things, it is stated that there will be €200 million per year from next year onwards for the next ten years to make Austria a lot safer in the field of flood protection. The situation of the air has also improved in Austria. The air quality has constantly improved in recent years. We also invest a lot in air content measures. This is also reflected in the environmental report. – For example, the Immission Protection Act Air: We have also adopted regulations on fine dust and nitrogen oxide here. For example, we have very strong reductions in air pollutants, in sulphur dioxide – think of acid rain, which is no longer a topic today. We also have significant reductions in ammonia or arsenic, lead, carbon monoxide and similar pollutants. We need action on nitrogen oxide and also on greenhouse gases. This is clearly where we need to be better. This means climate protection: Since this year, in 2013, we have been in a new climate period, namely by 2020. The climate and energy targets are clear, Austria needs to improve climate protection. We also have a chance. We have also innovationed climate protection here in the Hohe Haus. We have determined how much we can emit in transport, industry, agriculture, space heating by 2020 and how much we need to reduce greenhouse gases. The aim is to achieve our goals in climate protection, this time in the new period. We have recently decided on a package of measures in the Council of Ministers. We, the Federal Offices with the federal states, have made up the way we reduce greenhouse gases in transport, as we do in the economy and in other sectors. In parallel, we are working on the implementation of the energy transition, away from fossil energy sources. Our goal by 2020 is a share of 34 percent of renewable energy sources. We are now at a level of 31 percent. So we have consistently expanded this and will continue to do so. Many laws are used to implement this – eco electricity law, climate protection law and support measures. Waste management: Austria has a leading position in waste management across Europe. This is also confirmed by the Environmental Control Report. The amount of waste has fallen and we take a lot back into the fabric cycle. It is also positive that we want to reduce food waste. I'll invite you all to join us. A topic for the economy or resource management is also to save energy and raw materials. Here the Environmental Control Report confirms that material consumption has fallen by 12 percent and resource efficiency has increased by 13 percent. That means that this is also an important point. In conclusion: We are very good at environmental protection. The Environmental Control Report confirms the high level of the environment. We need to get better, and we work on it every day.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e24735	Mr President. Minister! High house! The central part of the novelle is the regulation that municipalities and private waste disposal companies will also be able to conclude contracts with competitors in the future. An important point in this innovation is also the obligation to participate in the collection and recovery of household packaging. Finally, I would like to stress that Austria is certainly playing a pioneering role in the collection, separation and disposal of waste. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e25023	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! In this House, major steps were taken in December 2012 Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to thank the rapporteur for his excellent report. A request was made across all party borders, with all the reasonable forces involved, and in December 2012 this overall request was also submitted here in this House to a decision-making body. This was a big step in the right direction, since from Austria all nuclear power operators have gone through the signal that it will be difficult in the future to talk about the nuclear question, import nuclear power, fail to properly conduct nuclear power plants in the area near the border, without including all related questions in a corresponding form of a solution. For example, we have been subject to all legal and political measures against an extension of the Czech Ministry of the Environment for blocks 3 and 4 of the Temelín AKW. We have also expressed our opinion that the EURATOM Treaty is to be a conference and that it will decide on the future of the EURATOM Treaty. Our opinion on this is – and this diverges with the view of the Greens – that is in a democracy, but I am particularly sorry that there has been no agreement in this case – that the EURATOM Treaty is no longer in its present form – as the colleague Lichtenecker has formulated in her application – and that it is intended to be further developed and dissolved and that the money paid by the Republic of Austria should be invested in alternative energy forms. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Unfortunately, this distinguishes us in this question. But if it were to succeed in finding a common consensus here, I would be glad. I would like to thank Mr Weninger very much, above all, for drawing the threads between all political groups in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, and for trying to bring them all to a table. Although it was not easy, it nevertheless succeeded to a large extent. Thank you once again. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I would also like to thank the environmental speaker of the ÖVP very much for the fact that there has also been a consensus here today to present this proposal for decision-making, and also for all the others who want to vote here today. I should like to say to the Greens that we have a different opinion on the EURATOM Treaty. The further development of the EURATOM Treaty – as you, Mrs Lichtenecker, who entered into your application at the same time – is not possible in our view, either, because it would contradict the statutes of the current EURATOM Treaty. That is why it was also our decision to find another way. I think it is a good way for Austria, for the anti-atomic policy and for the possibility of promoting alternative energies in the future.

We had 11,400 cubic meters of water in the second, and there were only about 40 centimetres left to pass. The fact is that the water, which no longer has space, has to be supplied somewhere. And first, you have to think about how this is going. People know that you also have to expect the worst serious case. And so that you can plan this and work it out wisely, it is necessary to talk about follow-up and subsequent compensation, because the damage can be enormous. We see this in the infrastructure damage visible today. Secondly, we are dealing with the question of how we can bring back water, which we leave out in absolute emergency. And thirdly, we are dealing with the question of the proper execution of a water transfer site so that a polder can be sent without damming disorder. These are dramatic requirements, and you can imagine what it would mean if, to the east of Vienna, the water that is now flowing through Vienna would actually run out. We might have a flood of up to 25,000 hectares, there would be a number of villages affected and some factories and many energy plants. And we can't risk that. On March, we only had a 200-year high water in the Retention Room March through the Danube, and we had no more danger there by expanding the dams. We see we people can do a lot, but we have to take care of them. Finally, at the end of this period, I should like to thank you for five years of cooperation. [[Abg. Oil: Yes, yes!]] I would have liked to see that we would have had a discussion in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, in the style of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, or perhaps in the style of another committee, of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. On nuclear applications, I can comment as follows: There has been an attempt on the part of Mr Weninger – I am also grateful for it – to make another joint request. But this request is that there are two points that can be supported, and one that cannot be supported. One point that can be supported is the same as my request. This is about preventing the further expansion of the Temelín nuclear power station. And the third point, EURATOM Treaty Review Conference – no no! But the second point is a point I cannot carry. For this reason, in the request, you would like to say that "the funds of the EURATOM community are reorientated and used exclusively in favour of the security aspects and the best possible protection of the population". Mr Schopf, Mr Weninger, I hope that this has already happened: to ensure the best protection of the population. That's what I'm going to assume. We don't need a request for that. What we want is that money no longer flows for EURATOM. It is up to €40 million a year, which are put in there – some speak of 80 million – only from Austria! Let's get out of here! We want to stop the final off of EURATOM, and there we are a little harder than the SPÖ together with the ÖVP and, with in the boat, the FPÖ. We want the final outcome, and we want these funds no longer to benefit in any form from the nuclear industry. Then: Where is it that one subsidizes a company so that it fulfils requirements? Every small farm in Austria has to do the same and does not receive subsidies, including from the EU. So that's what I'm talking about, like, equality with other businesses. That must be over, it must be radical! We want these funds to be used exclusively for renewable energies and energy efficiency technologies or storage technologies. That is what we want, and this is covered by the Greens' request, because we have already made a similar request for it recently. My request is very clear, and I hope to do so with the approval of all the parliamentary parties. This is because the EIA procedure for expanding the Temelín, blocks III and IV nuclear power stations has actually been carried out unlawfully in the EU. We therefore want to use all legal means to prevent this – keyword: Infringement procedures. We decided to do so in December 2012 in the principle paper, and you also have it in point 3 of your request. If you do not vote in favour, then you are making cheap party policy, which is not based on facts. But I assume that you will not do this in this important matter and will therefore support our request.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e25618	Mr Federal Minister Dipl.-Ing. Berlakovich. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e25639	Mr President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It is important that after floods, rapid help is provided to the people concerned but also that further protection is provided. Extreme floods often damage flood protection facilities. This must be quickly restored so that they can give people protection. This is what we have done in the large package that the Federal Government has decided as a consequence of the flood events. In fact, we have increased money for the repair of flood protection facilities and provided more money for the expansion of new flood protection facilities for the future, but also for the last repair of drinking water supply facilities, treatment plants and water supply facilities mentioned by the honourable Member Lettenbichler. There was a lot of damage. And we have also loosened money from the environmental and water management funds, € 20 million to invest quickly and help communities and countries repair these plants. It is important that people are quickly being supplied with drinking water again, that funds are made available for the water supply facilities, as well as waste water disposal facilities. This also helps the construction industry and is also part of economic measures for the regional construction industry. You know the current discussion that it is important to create and secure jobs. That is why this was an important step. Mr Klubobmann Lugar, you already have to look at the right data situation. Just as we consistently help with the flood here, this is also in the development of renewable energy. When I got into this function, we in Austria had a share of about 24, 25 percent renewable energy. Now we are at 31 percent and are on the right track to achieve the target 34 percent renewable energy in 2020. We have put in place a number of legal measures and have established support programmes. My home country Burgenland will be power-powered in this year's balance, because the possibilities have been used, as have other federal states, whether in the wind power, in the hydropower that plays an important role, but also in biomass and photovoltaics. And even in the case of hydropower, we do not say that each Berggsbacherl is to be demonstrated, but we have accelerated procedures because we have also decided here in the High House on the Environmental Impact Assessment Act Novelles, which has allowed us to achieve a reduction in the procedures. That they are being carried out more quickly and efficiently does not benefit anyone if much bureaucracy stands in the way. Approve or reject a project, but not endless procedures. Monitoring shows that the procedures are also shortened. – We do so in the environmental field. Special measures also in the field of thermal rehabilitation – you have mentioned this –: Despite the savings package, we have consistently set up €100 million to advance the thermal renovation, this year with an early bonus to help the regional construction industry. Those who opt for a thermal renovation get more money. Mr Mitterlehner and I have now extended this to flood-damaged people who are now restoring their homes. These get an additional bonus. Thus, we help and at the same time achieve our climate change objectives. Atom power: It's important that we come here closed to nuclear power. Austria has this position internationally. A few things are important. At the time, I initiated the stress tests for European nuclear power plants. For the first time, all European nuclear power plants have been reviewed, and it has been found that none is in order, but that there is a need for retrofitting everywhere. What has not yet been done is that the final report on these stress tests was discussed by the Heads of State or Government, namely that there are also consequences that can only be called: Shut down or retrofit nuclear power plants. It is therefore important for the Heads of State or Government to make a clear statement here. I criticize what Commissioner Oettinger has done, namely that he is now making stress tests in a slight variation. This is not a target, it is not enough, because nuclear power plants must continue to be subject to a strict examination. However, we have a completely different subject, namely that in Europe, in the face of energy scarcity – and energy is becoming more and more expensive – we are discussing re-enhancement of nuclear power. Under the term ‘technology neutrality’, states such as the UK and others want nuclear power to be equated with renewable energy. Austria rejects this. Nuclear power cannot be the answer, neither for climate protection nor for renewable energy objectives. But this is a political discussion, where we are at the beginning and which is also getting tougher. That is why it is It is all the more important that we remain consistent with this and that it is clear that nuclear power is not promoted. In this respect, it was important that we now also have the labelling of electricity. That was the result of the nuclear summit, which was also carried out here in Parliament, namely that we have the proof of origin for the electricity and that we prevent nuclear power from coming over the back door to Austria, so to speak. Therefore, at the end of the legislative period, I should also like to thank the members of the Committee on the Environment for the critical debate, for the positive discussion, partly also for the hard discussion. But the goal is clear: We all want to achieve together that we make Austria more viable in the environmental field. Thank you very much.

In the worst case, the same farmer has land in Styria and Carinthia, and then he cannot export these plant protection products under the same conditions in the two federal states. Each of us – including me as a farmer or at least at home – uses snail grain or the infamous roundup, and you don't need to prove as a private person where you use these things – as a farmer, however, you have to. ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e28870	Mr President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Austrian agriculture is known beyond its borders for its ecological orientation. The EU accession process attempts to take into account, in particular, the environmental component in Austrian agriculture. An environmental programme has been set up to strengthen organic farming and to reduce the use of means of production of fertilizers and plant protection products. We are being praised for this path in Europe, and this is also the way we are going and will continue now, because it has a perspective. On the other hand, it must not be forgotten that Austrian agriculture does not operate separately in free Europe, but is moving on completely liberalised markets and is therefore in a great competitive and competitive situation – let alone international trade. If the free trade agreement between Europe and the USA is currently being negotiated and hormones are used in agriculture in the United States, genetic engineering is used, and we do not want to do so – at least in Austria and in Europe – then we see how great the challenges are. Therefore, our agriculture is trying to go this way, to be a producing agriculture, to serve the markets, to cover the table of people, to produce sustainable food, to have at the same time an ecological orientation and to take into account the environment and climate. The effects of this environmental programme show that we are at the forefront of organic farming in Europe. No country in Europe is as strong in organic farming as we are, but conventional agriculture must also produce food, and its quality is very high. 90 percent of the people in Austria feed on Austrian conventional food. The effect of the environmental programme is that the use of fertilizers has been reduced by 33 percent since EU accession and the use of plant protection products has been reduced by 20 percent over the last 20 years. We must not forget that the farmers cost a lot of money. No one likes to use these resources, but they are needed to secure income and also to be able to work effectively for business reasons. Therefore, the ecological requirement is high, and that should also be so in the future. We also support the beekeepers – not only since today or tomorrow, but in the past we have supported the local bee industry, which supports beekeeping annually with € 1.5 million to € 2 million – through training measures, in particular with regard to the Varroa-Milben fight, and through the purchase of equipment. It is clear that both of us want: We want beekeepers who can work, bee protection, but also a agriculture that works. We have always sought this common path, and we want to go it in the future. That is why this bee programme is being expanded. Research will strengthen the loss of bees in terms of bees, and most importantly: In particular, support must be given to those farmers who now have to adapt to their production – many small farms, not only in the South Styria, but also in other regions. In particular, the Chamber of Agriculture is called upon to invest in educational measures to convert farmers there to new production techniques which are not yet available. Therefore, our concern must apply to the beekeeper, but very probably also to the farmers. – That was always the case, and we want to go this way in the future. I thank you for finding a common way to do more than other European countries, beyond the ban on the European Union. The ban starts earlier and continues longer. We are also extending the ban to other cultures, and in addition we are taking in an active substance that the European Union is first examining, namely the glyphosate. The European Union will present an evaluation in autumn. We start earlier with the ban on glyphosate, and also the offer in the food retail trade, where the consumer can easily buy it without being properly informed, should be prohibited, but it should only be available in the specialist trade, where consumers are also properly informed. In this sense, I would like to thank you for the broad consensus that guarantees that we have a producing agriculture with ecological responsibility. ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e29226	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! There is no doubt that bee protection is also human protection. Bees and other insects have a very ungrateful role in the ecosystem to be such an indicator because they react much earlier to pesticides, fungicides or herbicides than other organisms. In most mammals, toxins arrive much later in the organism. But, of course, large mammals, such as humans, are by no means immune or damage due to any plant protection products. A sad proof of this is, for example, the fate of some football players in Italy who, despite healthy, partly even ascetic lifestyles, suffer from a terrible disease, namely ALS. This is a creeping nervous disease that leads to paralysis – first the legs, then the hands, then the vocal cords, until then everything is paralysed on the body and a much earlier death occurs. There is quite massive evidence that this disease causes ALS from pesticides used in football courts in Italy. I think that in the discussion that we are having, it should not be a question of just having in mind how to increase the yield of agriculture as far as possible, but it should really be about how we can survive on this earth in such a way that everyone can survive. I believe that the right approach should be to find out first how various resources work on organisms, on beneficials, on pests and how to use them in such a way as to keep the environment in balance as possible. Everything else, the use of means, of which we do not really know exactly how they work, is biocid in the truest sense of the word. In any case, we people should not become pests of this earth. Thank you.

With the reform of criminal proceedings, the public prosecutorship as a hub in the investigation procedure has become the focus of the observation and, of course, also in some criticism. We have worked in detail in the Ministry of Justice and in the Subcommittee of the Justice Committee to evaluate the reform and have discussed in detail the effects and some developments in the implementation of this reform. It is important to me, however, to stress that the experts who have been heard have unanimously supported the structure and objectives of the reform. It is also equally important and mandated to the next legislative period, of course, that further agreement be reached on those topics that still need clarification or intensification in order to further develop the reform in a positive direction. High house! Let me briefly come to the most important areas of the documents to be decided today. In particular, within the meaning of the report of the Committee on Justice on the Evaluation of Criminal Process Reform, we are strengthening the efficiency of legal protection in the investigation process through the commitment to decision-making times and by extending opposition possibilities to alleged infringements by the criminal police. Each accused person's claim to actually understand what he is accused of is to be guaranteed by an understandable and comprehensive legal order and extension of the rights to interpretation and written translation. With the amendments to the Law on judicial cooperation within the European Union, we are strengthening the efficiency of the support of the national prosecutors by EUROJUST. In this context, we are committed to speeding up legal assistance and enforcement of orders and investigations with the support of judicial authorities from other Member States. Ladies and gentlemen! In response to the incident in Josefstadt, I would like to highlight two points which are particularly important to me. Firstly, of course, this is the question: How can the victim best be supported? To that end, we need to highlight the situation of the victim. The second point is the execution of the penalty, in particular the execution of the youth penalty in general. Let me start with the situation of the victim. Of course, I regret what happened to the victim. And, of course, such a thing must not happen in an Austrian penitentiary! That's quite clear! But it is also clear that, unfortunately, we cannot reverse what has happened. And now it's about: How can you best support the victim? I would like to point out that as soon as the victim entrusted himself to a psychologist, he was responded to in the Josefstadt Justice Centre. The perpetrators were shown, and the victim was spatially separated from the perpetrators and immediately received psychological and psychiatric care. And then we took care of the victim's accommodation in a well-kept housing community after he was released from prison. Of course, we are also dealing with the question of compensation for damages, as has already been said. This will also be dealt with very unbureaucratically and quickly. I told the victim all this in a letter. Of course, the second aspect you have to highlight is the question: What is it generally like in the criminal proceedings, especially in the execution of the youth penalty? And I would also like to point out, also in the context of the work done daily in criminal proceedings, that everything is really done to prevent such incidents. The employees in criminal proceedings do great work, do really difficult work every day in a difficult working environment, and they try hard to ensure that such incidents do not happen. But, of course, we also need to see that there is a constant improvement in judicial institutions, that new measures are being taken on a regular basis to prevent such incidents. Of course, the case we are talking about here was also the occasion to think about new measures. I've already been on the 25th. In June 2013, the following emergency measures were launched: Two-way detention rooms in the Youth Department. I have given the order that, as far as is possible, only two occupations actually take place in the cells in the youth department. Secondly, I have also ordered the installation of an employment firm in the youth department so that a day structure can be created and full employment can also be guaranteed. In general, I would like to stress that it is particularly important to me that young people really have the opportunity to re-enter a school degree, for example, because it is especially important for young people, especially young people, that they really have a second chance that they have the opportunity to re-socialise. That is why we are taking a number of measures to meet this objective. Finally, I have extended the time spent by staff members of the Vienna Jugendgerichtshilfe in the extended service, since the Vienna Jugendgerichtshilfe is now relieved because part of the activities is taken over by the family court. However, the Vienna Jugendgerichtshilfe should not be reduced, but it should now be able to bring these capacities even further in the Jugendprüfung. Finally, I would also like to point out that I have set up a task force on detention and possible alternatives, in the light of the fact that, of course, in the case of young people, the investigative detention is the absolute last resort. In this task force, we are thinking about what alternatives there can be to the U-port for young people. Mr Grosz pointed out that confidence in justice had suffered. Mr Grosz, I can calm you down: Trust in justice has increased. So you don't have to worry about this. I commissioned two trust studies on justice. [[Abg. Grosz: Has cost a lot of money again!]] At the end of 2011, the population’s confidence in justice was still 65 percent; At the beginning of 2013, Austrians' confidence in justice was 72 percent. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] A number of measures have been taken, but, of course, the very good work of the judges, prosecutors and prosecutors has also convinced the population and encouraged the confidence of the people in justice. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e31491	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! ==References====External links== On February 1, 2013, the European Court of Human Rights sentenced the European Court of Human Rights because, unlike unmarried heterosexual couples, the same-sex partners do not have a legal opportunity to adopt a physical child of the other partner or partner. The European Court of Human Rights has considered this legal situation to be illegal. It has already been pointed out that in Austria the European Convention on Human Rights is in the constitutional order. Austria is therefore obliged to adapt the current legal situation to the legal status of the European Court of Human Rights. Otherwise, there would be illegality of the constitution. The adoption right amendment law 2013 now implements the recognition of the EGMR. This means that it is thus possible to adopt the stepchild, i.e. the adoption of the partner's physical child or the partner. It is important to me, however, to note that a stepchild adoption by a gender partner must, of course, be examined and approved by the court as well as any other adoption. Of course, child welfare plays an important, yes, the most important role. In addition, the other physical parent of the adoption must agree. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, let me conclude by referring briefly to the special status of marriage in relation to the right of adoption. This special position remains secure in the future. The adoption of a foreign child by several persons remains reserved for spouses. This also applies to the successive adoption, i.e. the adoption of a child already adopted by the partner or the partner. These are also reserved for spouses. I am also – as some Members have already mentioned – the firm conviction that it is the best for a child when it has both sexes of father and mother and therefore referees. As far as possible, this ideal case should also be remodelled with an adoption. Therefore, the marriage continues to enjoy a special position in the Austrian adoption law, as well as the child's well-being. I'd like to explain one thing here. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: Do you say why the sex plays a role?]] Let me clarify this. [[Abg. Dr. Matznetter: You did not answer the question!]] It was repeatedly discussed why there is also the possibility of individual adoption. There's one thing you need to know: A constituency replaces the physical father or an electoral mother replaces the physical mother, but the family relations with the other physical parent remain upright! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e32060	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Today, we will limit § 278a StGB – known as “Mafia-Paragraph” – to its core of regulation, thus giving it the regulation purpose originally meant by the ratio legis. In this way we follow a recommendation by Professor Krauskopf, who gave her the Federal Ministry's evaluation in the context of the evaluation mandated by Parliament. In order to get to this present presentation, there has been a lot of work to do in recent weeks, i.e. the typical parliamentary bonework or the drilling of hard boards – as Max Weber has well formulated. In particular, the fears were to be scattered that, without the previous regulation, illegal behaviour, in particular radical animal protectionists, would no longer be prosecuted by criminal law. I am very pleased that this has been successful and that the joint conviction work has paid off. In this context, I am expressly grateful for the support and cooperation of the Federal Minister for Justice, Karl, especially Mr Hannes Jarolim and Mr Albert Steinhauser, and also the club members of ÖVP and SPÖ. However, I would like to make the following request to the Committee on Justice's report: [[Quoted print version removed]] The reason for the innovation of “terror financing” is that we want to implement a recommendation from FATF into the substantive criminal law – we already have one part, the other part we regulate with it. In terms of content, on the one hand, we want to increase the level of punishment, on the other hand also punish the financing of individual terrorists in the future and increase the criminality of organised terrorist financing. It is decisive – and with this I conclude – that in subjective terms the knowledge is a prerequisite of criminality. The perpetrator must therefore know that a terrorist crime is committed or that the association is directed towards the conduct of such crimes. With this we take this § 278d very precise and do not run the risk that an overwhelming interpretation can take place.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e33478	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I can't spare this criticism to this government at a later hour: it didn't do anything in connection with copyright. We know that this is a very difficult issue, but a government is there to solve difficult issues, and this has not happened during this legislative period. I now mention only five points in which I believe that copyright is urgently reformed and innovative. [[Abg. Krainer: We all want to hear!]] Firstly, legal certainty. We have about eight million copyright infringements a day in this country, and a large part of them are caused by the fact that people do not know that they are doing these copyright infringements. It is therefore necessary to establish legal certainty. Secondly, The authors no longer come to their due revenue. That means there is also a need for innovation. Thirdly, contract law. The artists are in a very stupid situation when they cannot conclude contracts with large societies such as the radio stations, film production companies and the like, which grant them the most necessary rights. Fourthly, the free use of protected works. We live in times of cultural change, which makes it possible not only to consume, but also to participate actively in the production process. And in the end, that means that no one cares great about copyright. Fifth, the reform of collecting societies. These include transparent design and democratisation. And so on. These were just five examples, and nothing happened. For this, however, ladies and gentlemen, we now have this innovation, which actually does not mean anything else than extending the term of protection from 50 to 70 years for record producers and performances on sound mediums – and this is a bowing towards the majors, towards the large industry in the music business. I can tell you, Mrs Minister – and I cannot spare you this accusation: That wouldn't have been necessary. Mrs Minister, you said in the Committee that you voted against it in the Council of the EU, and I can only report this – we have researched it –: That's not true. Austria has abstained from the vote, and that means that together with Lithuania you could have led to a turn, but have bowed to a power made up of the large countries. There you were just too cowardly. In fact, they could have brought about a trend change. This extension and extension of the term of protection does not at all serve the artists, does not at all serve the authors; it serves only the music industry. And it would have been easy to change something. I believe that it is necessary to consider, in general, whether Austria always needs to follow up on the big countries, if they have any proposals, or if one cannot do with the many other small countries occasionally, which also benefits citizens, in this case the authors. I think that in the next legislative period, there will be an opportunity with us to innovation copyright; I hope so. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-07-05-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00216_d8e33536	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In view of the late hour, I'm going to take a short look. The Austrian Copyright Act refers to the actual copyright and the so-called related rights of protection. Works in the sense of copyright law are peculiar spiritual creations in the fields of literature, sound art, visual arts and film art. The intellectual property of the copyright is to be protected by copyright. Unfortunately, until now, the conflicting interests of the authors on the one hand and consumers, on the other hand, have failed to bring the Internet user into a hat or also to give the collecting companies the appropriate legal framework. The present proposal is therefore essentially limited to implementing the corresponding EU Directive on the term of protection of copyright and related rights. It is about the duration of the period of protection from 50 – you have already heard – to increase to 70 years after the first release. We must take this decision today. The EU has instructed the Austrian legislator to issue this Directive until 1. ==References====External links== Without the mandatory requirement of the Directive, the law also states that the extension of the term of protection by this Federal Act does not justify an increase in the rates of collecting societies for remuneration or a change in the distribution of revenue from these remunerations between different right holders. In the interest of artists and authors, it would be very important for us to achieve a truly fundamental reform of this copyright in the next legislative period. I'm optimistic. - Thank you.

We have presented here a novel of the University Law which, first of all, corresponds to the government programme, which is the possibility of mergers of universities. – It is said that synergies and cooperation should be useful and brought about, up to mergers of universities. Such associations of universities are an international trend, you can see that in Denmark, you can see that in Germany, you can see that in France and in other countries. The reason is that in some cases, in the university field, a merger often leads to the necessary critical size – there are many constellations where a certain size of advantage is a prerequisite for high quality. Mergers are pushing But also for other reasons on or should they be at least possible. This is the purpose of this novel: To allow mergers, namely, to enable them in two forms, namely, by allowing universities themselves to become an initiative, and, on the other hand, – which is of course and can never be the subject of criticism – by, of course, each minister can try to apply for and enforce a corresponding law in Parliament. This is also a matter of course foreseen. We then also arrange some details of how such a combination should take place when it is possible to make it. But above all, we also regulate – and this is the second part of this law – the possibility of creating medical faculties. This can be interpreted as “Lex Linz”, but it is not. For example, if one medical university is associated with another, it will need appropriate regulations. All this, I think, is sensible, is true, and it's also time for you to do it. A second proposal to be adopted today is the Education Agreement between Austria and Brazil. I was in Brazil a few weeks ago and have prepared this agreement there. Brazil, we all know, is a very emerging state in South America. We have very good trade relations with him – he is Austria's largest economic partner in South America – and above all: Brazil has a great interest in making its students more mobile and sending them abroad, and Austria has been – I have said this several times – a sought-after partner. That is why it is good to have a corresponding agreement here. These points are on. At the end of this legislative period and at the end of my cooperation with the High House in this GP, I would like to thank all those who cooperated with me. Above all, I would like to thank you, dear Katharina Cortolezis-Schlager: Working with you was excellent, constructive. Thank you very much! I think we have, in the sense of the motto viribus unitis, shared a lot of things: from the University Quality Assurance Act to the Educational Law to many other important innovations. I am glad about that and thank you very much for that.

At the end of a meeting, it is common – I do not now – to hold a final address by the President, earlier the President, because it is also the legislative period that ends. I have allowed myself to send you my final address in writing. [[General condolences.]] There are recognizable numbers in there. I think you can use them well. The only thing I do not have in this letter, which I would like to say here orally, is that I would like to thank all our employees in the Parliamentary Directorate, in which clubs very much the parliamentary staff, very much for their intensive work through five years. [[General condolences.]] I should also like to thank my two alternates, Mr Neugebauer, Mr Graf, and, of course, the club members, but also the vice-presidents, who have always been present in the Presidency, for the good cooperation, for collaborative cooperation. We've got it, I think, in business order. I wish you all the best! Thank you. [[General condolences.]] The sitting is closed.
